I guess… the main thing is my eating got really messed up. Like, I used to track everything—calories, macros, the whole spreadsheet thing, which sounds ridiculous, but I’m in project management so I kind of track everything. Even my grocery list used to have categories.

No, not really like… I wasn’t thinking, “Oh, I can’t gain weight.” It was more like food just stopped being on the list somehow. Which is weird because I used to track everything—calories, macros, all of it, like a work dashboard almost. I’m usually very organized with that stuff.

But I was crying over them, just staring at my inbox like it was this huge thing I couldn’t manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of what happened with the sponsor at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s when it really hit, or at least that’s the point I keep coming back to. I don’t really know if it’s still “going on” in the same way. Work is still there, obviously, and people still send messages and there are meetings, but I’ve kind of lost track of what counts as the situation itself versus just… the whole mess around it.

I haven’t really wanted to hike, which is weird because normally that clears my head. And my camera’s just been sitting there—I usually edit photos at night, like I’ll get obsessive about color and light and all that, but I haven’t even opened the files.\n\nEven the woodworking project in my garage… I keep walking past it and thinking I should do something, but I just don’t touch it. Same with logging meals, which I used to do all the time. It’s like the things that usually keep me grounded just feel… far away.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been about three months, I think. Since that whole project thing blew up, or since the sponsor questioned me—around then. It didn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m sad today,” it just kind of settled in.\n\nI kept thinking it would pass after a weekend, like if I cleaned the apartment or got through the next set of meetings, but it’s just… dragged on. The hollow, ashamed feeling, not wanting to do stuff—yeah, about three months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual?\n\nPatient: Less, I think… or not even less exactly, it’s more like bad sleep. I’ll try to go to bed, and I’m exhausted, but then I keep waking up at these weird hours, like 2:40 or 4:10, and my brain is right back in the deployment plan.\n\nLike I’ll be lying there thinking about some dependency or timeline thing, and then I remember I forgot to buy dish soap or there’s a box in the hallway I still haven’t broken down—sorry, that’s not really the point. By morning I feel wrecked, not rested. Like I technically slept in pieces, but it doesn’t feel like sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying that, but yes. I started thinking people at work would honestly be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I wasn’t there making everything worse or slowing things down or being this… problem everyone has to manage.\n\nAnd after that meeting—sorry, the one where everything felt like it collapsed—I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it. I wasn’t trying to be dramatic, it just came out, and then I kind of scared myself because it sounded too real.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period like this where you felt depressed and lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks before, like when work got messy or after deadlines, but weeks and weeks where I completely lost interest in everything… I’m not sure.\n\nI don’t track it very well, honestly. Time kind of blurs together for me—like I’ll remember what project I was on, or what apartment I was in, but not how I felt every day. There were times I was down, yeah, but whether it was like this? I don’t know. This feels… heavier than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed and lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I keep trying to line it up in my head, like “was there a two-week stretch,” but I don’t really have it organized that way.\n\nThere were times I was definitely low, like I’d go quiet or stop doing much outside of work, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day or if I just remember the bad parts because they stand out. It’s weird, because I can remember specific offices I worked in or what software rollout was happening, but not whether I felt that way every day for two weeks.\n\nSo… I can’t say yes for sure. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: At any time before this current period, have you ever had two weeks in a row when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, before all this, the clearest thing I can think of was after the sponsor questioned my judgment. It was like the bottom just dropped out.\n\nI remember sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I was crying over emails that normally I would’ve just handled. Which is stupid, because they were just emails, you know? I used to be good at keeping that stuff contained.\n\nWhether that was exactly two weeks in a row, I… I don’t know. I’m bad with the timeline. Work is still there, obviously, and people still send messages and there are meetings, but I’ve kind of lost track of what counts as the situation itself versus just… the whole mess around it.\n\nIt’s like when a project gets delayed and then suddenly the delay becomes the project, if that makes sense. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… before these past three months, the time I keep thinking of is after the sponsor questioned my judgment. I know I’ve brought it up already, but it really did something to me.

It wasn’t just being annoyed or embarrassed at work. It was like the bottom dropped out. I’d be in my apartment just feeling hollow and ashamed, and then I’d open emails—emails I normally would’ve handled, because that’s literally what I do all day—and I’d start crying.

And then I’d get mad at myself for crying, because it’s like, come on, it’s just an email. But at that time it felt like everything in me just collapsed. So… yeah, that’s the other time I remember feeling that way nearly every day.

I don’t know. This feels… heavier than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed and lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. That kind of scared me, because I hadn’t been trying to do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes. After the sponsor questioned my judgment at work, it was like—I don’t know—the bottom just dropped out. I went home and just sat in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed.\n\nAnd it’s stupid because normally I can handle emails, even tense ones. That’s literally part of my job. But I was crying over them, just staring at my inbox like it was this huge thing I couldn’t manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this started because of what happened with the sponsor at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s when it really hit, or at least that’s the point I keep coming back to. When the sponsor questioned my judgment, it was like the bottom just dropped out. I went home and sat in my apartment feeling hollow and embarrassed, like I’d messed up something obvious.\n\nAnd I know people question decisions all the time at work—there are whole meetings about it, and usually I can handle that stuff. I’ve had worse email threads, honestly. But after that, even normal emails felt impossible. I was just sitting there crying over messages I normally would’ve answered in two minutes.\n\nDoctor: Besides work, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… outside of work too. I haven’t really wanted to hike, which is weird because normally that clears my head. And my camera’s just been sitting there—I usually edit photos at night, like I’ll get obsessive about color and light and all that, but I haven’t even opened the files.\n\nEven the woodworking project in my garage… I keep walking past it and thinking I should do something, but I just don’t touch it. Same with logging meals, which I used to do all the time. It’s like the things that usually keep me grounded just feel… far away.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been about three months, I think. Since that whole project thing blew up, or since the sponsor questioned me—around then. It didn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m sad today,” it just kind of settled in.\n\nI kept thinking it would pass after a weekend, like if I cleaned the apartment or got through the next set of meetings, but it’s just… dragged on. The hollow, ashamed feeling, not wanting to do stuff—yeah, about three months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual?\n\nPatient: Less, I think… or not even less exactly, it’s more like bad sleep. I’ll try to go to bed, and I’m exhausted, but then I keep waking up at these weird hours, like 2:40 or 4:10, and my brain is right back in the deployment plan.\n\nLike I’ll be lying there thinking about some dependency or timeline thing, and then I remember I forgot to buy dish soap or there’s a box in the hallway I still haven’t broken down—sorry, that’s not really the point. By morning I feel wrecked, not rested. Like I technically slept in pieces, but it doesn’t feel like sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying that, but yes. I started thinking people at work would honestly be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I wasn’t there making everything worse or slowing things down or being this… problem everyone has to manage.\n\nAnd after that meeting—sorry, the one where everything felt like it collapsed—I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it. I wasn’t trying to be dramatic, it just came out, and then I kind of scared myself because it sounded too real.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period like this where you felt depressed and lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks before, like when work got messy or after deadlines, but weeks and weeks where I completely lost interest in everything… I’m not sure.\n\nI don’t track it very well, honestly. Time kind of blurs together for me—like I’ll remember what project I was on, or what apartment I was in, but not how I felt every day. There were times I was down, yeah, but whether it was like this? I don’t know. This feels… heavier than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed and lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I keep trying to line it up in my head, like “was there a two-week stretch,” but I don’t really have it organized that way.\n\nThere were times I was definitely low, like I’d go quiet or stop doing much outside of work, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day or if I just remember the bad parts because they stand out. It’s weird, because I can remember specific offices I worked in or what software rollout was happening, but not whether I felt that way every day for two weeks.\n\nSo… I can’t say yes for sure. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: At any time before this current period, have you ever had two weeks in a row when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, before all this, the clearest thing I can think of was after the sponsor questioned my judgment. It was like the bottom just dropped out.\n\nI remember sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I was crying over emails that normally I would’ve just handled. Which is stupid, because they were just emails, you know? I used to be good at keeping that stuff contained.\n\nWhether that was exactly two weeks in a row, I… I don’t know. I’m bad with the timeline. But that period stands out as really low.\n\nDoctor: Is the work situation that started this still going on?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s still “going on” in the same way. Work is still there, obviously, and people still send messages and there are meetings, but I’ve kind of lost track of what counts as the situation itself versus just… the whole mess around it.\n\nIt’s like when a project gets delayed and then suddenly the delay becomes the project, if that makes sense. You start with one issue, and then everyone has a spreadsheet about the issue, and a meeting about the spreadsheet, and then nobody remembers what the original decision was. That’s sort of how it feels in my head.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I haven’t really gotten a clear ending to it, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the one I keep coming back to is after the sponsor questioned my judgment. That really knocked something loose, I guess.\n\nI was just in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d start crying over emails that normally I would’ve just dealt with. Like, I’ve handled way worse work stuff before, rollout problems, budget fights, people changing scope at the last second—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nBut yeah, that period felt like the bottom dropped out. Whether I can prove it was exactly two weeks, I’m not great with that part, but it felt pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, do you remember ever having another time in your life when you felt this depressed for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve said it, but the main one I remember was after the sponsor questioned my judgment. That just—something about it made everything collapse for me.\n\nI’d be sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d start crying over emails. Which sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because they were just emails, and normally I’m the person who handles that stuff and keeps moving. But at that time it felt like I couldn’t.\n\nI’m not perfect on whether it was exactly two weeks, timelines get blurry for me, but that’s the period that comes to mind when you ask.\n\nDoctor: Do these reactions feel much stronger than you would normally expect from being questioned at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… honestly, yes. I mean, people get questioned at work all the time, especially in project management. Sponsors push back, people doubt decisions, that’s almost part of the job.\n\nBut this didn’t feel like normal embarrassment or frustration. After he questioned my judgment, it was like the bottom dropped out. I was sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d cry over emails I normally would’ve just answered and moved on from.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, because it’s work, it’s not like—well, I don’t know. But yeah, it felt way stronger than what should’ve happened.\n\nDoctor: Before this started three months ago, do you remember ever having another period in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. The timing is a little fuzzy, but it felt like it was nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before these past three months, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… before these past three months, the time I keep thinking of is after the sponsor questioned my judgment. And my camera’s just been sitting there—I usually edit photos at night, like I’ll get obsessive about color and light and all that, but I haven’t even opened the files.\n\nEven the woodworking project in my garage… I keep walking past it and thinking I should do something, but I just don’t touch it. Same with logging meals, which I used to do all the time. It’s like the things that usually keep me grounded just feel… far away.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been about three months, I think. Since that whole project thing blew up, or since the sponsor questioned me—around then. It didn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m sad today,” it just kind of settled in.\n\nI kept thinking it would pass after a weekend, like if I cleaned the apartment or got through the next set of meetings, but it’s just… dragged on. The hollow, ashamed feeling, not wanting to do stuff—yeah, about three months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual?\n\nPatient: Less, I think… or not even less exactly, it’s more like bad sleep. I’ll try to go to bed, and I’m exhausted, but then I keep waking up at these weird hours, like 2:40 or 4:10, and my brain is right back in the deployment plan.\n\nLike I’ll be lying there thinking about some dependency or timeline thing, and then I remember I forgot to buy dish soap or there’s a box in the hallway I still haven’t broken down—sorry, that’s not really the point. By morning I feel wrecked, not rested. Like I technically slept in pieces, but it doesn’t feel like sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t like saying that, but yes. I started thinking people at work would honestly be better off if I just didn’t wake up. Like if I wasn’t there making everything worse or slowing things down or being this… problem everyone has to manage.\n\nAnd after that meeting—sorry, the one where everything felt like it collapsed—I actually said out loud that I should just walk into traffic and be done with it. I wasn’t trying to be dramatic, it just came out, and then I kind of scared myself because it sounded too real.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, have you ever had another period like this where you felt depressed and lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve had bad weeks before, like when work got messy or after deadlines, but weeks and weeks where I completely lost interest in everything… I’m not sure.\n\nI don’t track it very well, honestly. Time kind of blurs together for me—like I’ll remember what project I was on, or what apartment I was in, but not how I felt every day. There were times I was down, yeah, but whether it was like this? I don’t know. This feels… heavier than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed and lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I keep trying to line it up in my head, like “was there a two-week stretch,” but I don’t really have it organized that way.\n\nThere were times I was definitely low, like I’d go quiet or stop doing much outside of work, but I don’t know if it was nearly every day or if I just remember the bad parts because they stand out. It’s weird, because I can remember specific offices I worked in or what software rollout was happening, but not whether I felt that way every day for two weeks.\n\nSo… I can’t say yes for sure. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: At any time before this current period, have you ever had two weeks in a row when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, before all this, the clearest thing I can think of was after the sponsor questioned my judgment. It was like the bottom just dropped out.\n\nI remember sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I was crying over emails that normally I would’ve just handled. Which is stupid, because they were just emails, you know? I used to be good at keeping that stuff contained.\n\nWhether that was exactly two weeks in a row, I… I don’t know. I’m bad with the timeline. But that period stands out as really low.\n\nDoctor: Is the work situation that started this still going on?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s still “going on” in the same way. Work is still there, obviously, and people still send messages and there are meetings, but I’ve kind of lost track of what counts as the situation itself versus just… the whole mess around it.\n\nIt’s like when a project gets delayed and then suddenly the delay becomes the project, if that makes sense. You start with one issue, and then everyone has a spreadsheet about the issue, and a meeting about the spreadsheet, and then nobody remembers what the original decision was. That’s sort of how it feels in my head.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. I haven’t really gotten a clear ending to it, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the one I keep coming back to is after the sponsor questioned my judgment. That really knocked something loose, I guess.\n\nI was just in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d start crying over emails that normally I would’ve just dealt with. Like, I’ve handled way worse work stuff before, rollout problems, budget fights, people changing scope at the last second—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nBut yeah, that period felt like the bottom dropped out. Whether I can prove it was exactly two weeks, I’m not great with that part, but it felt pretty constant.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, do you remember ever having another time in your life when you felt this depressed for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve said it, but the main one I remember was after the sponsor questioned my judgment. That just—something about it made everything collapse for me.\n\nI’d be sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d start crying over emails. Which sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because they were just emails, and normally I’m the person who handles that stuff and keeps moving. But at that time it felt like I couldn’t.\n\nI’m not perfect on whether it was exactly two weeks, timelines get blurry for me, but that’s the period that comes to mind when you ask.\n\nDoctor: Do these reactions feel much stronger than you would normally expect from being questioned at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… honestly, yes. I mean, people get questioned at work all the time, especially in project management. Sponsors push back, people doubt decisions, that’s almost part of the job.\n\nBut this didn’t feel like normal embarrassment or frustration. After he questioned my judgment, it was like the bottom dropped out. I was sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d cry over emails I normally would’ve just answered and moved on from.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, because it’s work, it’s not like—well, I don’t know. But yeah, it felt way stronger than what should’ve happened.\n\nDoctor: Before this started three months ago, do you remember ever having another period in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, the one I keep circling back to is after that sponsor questioned my judgment. It sounds like such a work thing, and I know people deal with that all the time, but for me it was like the bottom dropped out.\n\nI was just in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and I’d cry over emails I normally would’ve handled. Like, I usually can just respond, organize the mess, keep things moving—that’s kind of my job—but then I’d look at a message and just fall apart.\n\nSo, yeah, before this three-month thing, that’s the period I remember. The timing is a little fuzzy, but it felt like it was nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before these past three months, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… before these past three months, the time I keep thinking of is after the sponsor questioned my judgment. I know I’ve brought it up already, but it really did something to me.\n\nIt wasn’t just being annoyed or embarrassed at work. It was like the bottom dropped out. I’d be in my apartment just feeling hollow and ashamed, and then I’d open emails—emails I normally would’ve handled, because that’s literally what I do all day—and I’d start crying.\n\nAnd then I’d get mad at myself for crying, because it’s like, come on, it’s just an email. But at that time it felt like everything in me just collapsed. So… yeah, that’s the other time I remember feeling that way nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Before these past three months, was there another separate time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I keep coming back to the work thing, but that’s the separate time I remember most clearly. After that sponsor questioned my judgment, it was like something just dropped out from under me.\n\nI’d go home and just sit in my apartment feeling… hollow, and really ashamed. And it’s stupid because I’ve handled way worse emails, like budget escalations and people arguing over scope, all that normal project chaos—usually I can just organize it. But then I’d open an email I normally would’ve dealt with and I’d start crying.\n\nSo… yes, that’s the time I’d count. It felt like it was nearly every day for at least a couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Before this started three months ago, was there ever a different time in your life when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think… I mean, I know I keep landing on the same example, but that’s the different time that sticks out. After that sponsor questioned my judgment, it felt like the bottom just dropped out.\n\nI’d be sitting in my apartment feeling hollow and ashamed, and then I’d open some normal email—stuff I usually handle all day, like it’s my job—and I’d just start crying over it. Which made me feel even more ridiculous, because it was just emails, but I couldn’t get myself past it.\n\nDoctor: Before this episode, had you ever gone back to feeling mostly like yourself for at least two months before becoming this depressed again?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like… “mostly like myself” is hard for me to pin down, because I tend to think of it in terms of whether I was getting through work and answering people and not falling apart over every little thing.\n\nThere probably were stretches where I seemed more normal, I guess, but I don’t know if I can confidently say, “yes, that was two full months.” Time gets kind of blurry when I look back at it. I remember phases more than exact lengths."
